ParlaMint-AT_2000-03-21-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00016_d7e4646	Mr President of the Federal Republic of Germany! Mr President. Mr. Federal Chancellor! Mrs. Vice-Chancellor! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! High house! The budget of the year 2000 which I am allowed to present to you today is the first of the current legislative period. It is intended to replace the statutory budget procedure before the summer. This budget begins with the implementation of the joint declaration of government from February of this year. Ladies and gentlemen! Despite difficult framework conditions due to a threatening 109 billion schilling net deficit in the general budget, the new federal government has proven its ability to act and managed the 2000 budget at a record time of only four weeks. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] The commitment to fiscal consolidation entered into by Austria as a result of the Maastricht Treaty allows us to owe the federal government 62 billion Schilling. This is 2.2 percent of the gross domestic product and 1.7 percent for the overall state. The draft for the 2000 Federal Estimate provides revenue of 726.7 billion schilling and expenditure of 781.3 billion schilling, thus providing an administrative net deficit of around 54.6 billion schilling or 2% of the gross domestic product. This is because the Maastricht deficit will only be achieved in 2000 if the administrative deficit is significantly below the target of 62 billion schilling. This has been achieved by the present proposal of the Federal Government. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] It should therefore be assumed that the commitments of the Federal Government from the Austrian Stability Programme concerning the deficit target for 2000 can also be met. These figures also require that the countries, cities and municipalities can actually meet a target surplus of 0.5 percent of the gross domestic product. This is certainly an ambitious but quite attainable goal if all local authorities are to establish the fiscal consolidation of their budgets as a special priority. I can remind you that the starting point of the considerations was the forecast and addressed 109 billion schilling net deficit. Of the consolidation measures in 2000, 14.5 billion shilling is now due to expenditure savings, only around 7 billion shilling is achieved by increases in taxes and fees, 20 billion shilling by one-off measures and 12.9 billion shilling by fund levies. [[Abg. Parnigoni: Increases! Increases! I always hear increases!]] If one compares the net deficit as a percentage of the gross domestic product to the deficits of past years, then last time in 1974 – in 1974, ladies and gentlemen – it has managed to reduce the value of 2% of the administrative budget in 2000. In absolute terms, the net deficit of the year 2000 was last lowered by 46.6 billion schilling in 1982. This path towards the reorganisation of our public budget, in particular, needs to be continued in the next few years, must be strengthened in the structural area in order to create budgetary spaces necessary for the financing and modernisation of vital and sustainable structures in our country. As the financial year 2000 had already been advanced, deeper structural measures could not yet be taken. ParlaMint-AT_2000-03-21-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00016_d7e5969	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! Mr President of the Court of Auditors. Mr. State Secretary! What the Court of Auditors noted here, and I too would like to thank him very much, is really not just the guidance for future volumes of measures, but that is more or less a register of 10 commandments: How do I do it neatly? What was criticized? – First of all: The word "equal opportunities" is widespread, is widespread, is in all mouths, is almost at risk of being used inflationaryly. Equal opportunities will come into force when you know what it is all about, when you define precisely what it is all about, and if you also have implementation strategies and assessment tools as a whole. This is exactly what the Court of Auditors said about women's promotion, as it was provided by the labour market service. I think it was very correct, and I hope it will also be able to use appropriate levers to ensure that the labour market service and, in addition, other institutions from the vocabulary "equal opportunities" finally arrive at precise, concrete projects and, above all, offer opportunities to evaluate what has been done with regard to equal opportunities. I therefore come to a second point of criticism in this area, which I would like to stress and present: the controlling – which my previous speaker has already mentioned – as a deficit in this field of promotion, especially in this field of labour market service. It has been confirmed that the internal classification and the time continuity of the target control and the structured focus are actually missing. And without these conditions it is very, very difficult to examine the funding that has been allocated to the benefit of women for their efficiency. This leaves us in a vague space, so we move again on an uncertain level, and this uses some means, without ensuring that they really bring what they should bring long ago. Therefore, I would like to stress once again the demand for a better controlling, which also requires that we have an appropriate data base. In this respect, the AMS – in the past, I emphasize – has been still sparse. The relevant computer systems were missing, and the data base is not yet such that it is now possible to make concrete positive or negative comments on the efficiency of women's promotion. A third criticism, which is to be addressed in many areas, particularly in the area of subsidies, is the lack of longer-term planning, the lack of longer-term programmes, the so-called short-term disbursement, the one-year grant of subsidies, and the lack of a plan that covers almost four years. There is certainly no blame for the AMS in the first place, but there is the client, namely to take the Federal Government more or less into his prayer and also to take responsibility. It is precisely the task of a new federal government not to repeat the mistakes of the old, but to ensure real planning security and also to carry out longer-term planning. I therefore come to the fourth point that the Court of Auditors is criticizing, namely, the resources used for the qualification of women. Qualification programmes are very important especially for re-entry, and qualification programmes are also the opportunity for women to be able to perceive equal opportunities in the professional field in concrete terms, even if they are offered at all. And just one third of the qualification programmes have benefited women, two thirds of the qualification programmes have benefited men. This is a miscontrol which, in view of the needs, really goes beyond the problem, and especially in the case of this miscontrol, it must be assumed: away with the casting can, towards a targeted rate of support towards women who are employed in the so-called less qualified area, towards women who have to re-enter. In this respect, it is still very important to mention what the Court of Auditors also calls for, namely the provision of childcare facilities. I can take part in this report and I would like to deal with the zero coupon fund in my statement. This zero coupon fund was founded in 1986. The reason for the establishment of this fund was the zero coupon bonds issued by the Federal Government for the first time in 1985, which, as we know, are issued interest-rately and cancelled at fair value. In August 1997, the resolution of this fund was finally passed by law on 1 August 1997. ==References== The Austrian Federal Finance Agency had the task of disbanding allegations made for the zero coupon fund in the best possible way and of making these financial resources available to the federal government by the end of August 1998. We know, however, how the Court of Auditors report shows that this fund has been dissolved in advance. On the basis of this early resolution, the Court of Auditors' review took place in November and December 1998. There are critical statements by the Court of Auditors in this regard. I would like to draw particular attention to one point: As mentioned above, the sale of securities took place about five months before the legal settlement. It is remarkable that this sale took place even before the due date of the coupon. We all know when the coupon is charged, then unit interest is charged, and this, of course, causes damage. In the course of this transaction, a net damage of about 86.2 million schilling was ultimately incurred. The second criticism: The resolution of the Fund – as we have already heard today – is expected to result in significant additional burden on the future budgets, namely around 1.37 billion schilling in 2000, 537 million schilling in 2002 and 171 million schilling in 2007, and in 2016 it is finally 11.3 billion schilling. However, I would also like to point out some positive aspects mentioned in the Court of Auditors' report. The Fund was disbanded in 1997 and 1998 with considerable financial resources allocated to the Federal budget, in particular in 1997 1.2 billion schilling, in 1998 there were 4.7 billion schilling, in total 5.9 billion schilling. This amount, of course, supported the efforts of the Federal Government to consolidate the budget. In the end, this measure also served to achieve the Maastricht criteria. I would also like to point out something positive: Until the dissolution, the zero coupon fund was largely successful in investing donations and generating interest rates. In the years 1993 to 1995 interest rates and interest rates increased by around 75.5 million Schilling. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! I would also like to thank the ladies and gentlemen of the Court of Auditors very much, on behalf of my group, for the President-in-Office of the Council. We will be very happy to give the Court of Auditors' report of action acknowledgment.

The aim here was not to have to purchase any exchange rate losses in the resolution of the fund. ParlaMint-AT_2000-03-21-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00016_d7e10420	Mr President. Federal Minister! Mr President-in-Office of the Council! In my debate contribution to the Court of Auditors' report on the 1998 management of the labour market service and the improvement of women's opportunities on the labour market. However, the promotion of equal opportunities for women and men was an integral part of the AMS's definition of appropriate instruments for assessing the degree of equal opportunities achieved by the measures taken. In 1998, 50.5% of total labour market policy funding was allocated to women. The Court of Auditors considers this to be appropriate in view of the particular problems faced by women on the labour market and the higher unemployment rates compared to men. However, in the context of the special programme for re-entry women, the labour market service does not have comprehensible detailed data in terms of funding or the number of participants involved. The requirements for controlling are computer-based at all improveable. After 30 years of socialist social ministers, the situation of women on the labour market is still markedly different from men. On average, women have a lower level of qualification in Austria. Women achieve lower incomes. Women have less opportunities to climb than men. In order to disadvantage women in working life, the concentration on few occupations and the reduced mobility of women contributed. A special obstacle arose for women in connection with childcare and related duties. Children now only have women, and childcare is mostly female in Austria. No matter whether in the baby age, in the early childhood, in the school age or later: The educational work in Austria is about 80 percent of the women's business. This, of course, depends very much on the profession and employment. Of course, it makes a difference whether child rearing is a paid, a professional activity or whether it is a non-paid activity in the family association. The employment of women, i.e. the paid work of women, is thanks to God in getting up, but the interruption of this paid work is also much more common for women than for men, due to baby break, family care and educational work. The proportion of women in part-time employment, of minor employment, is much greater than in men, and the unemployment rate is also much higher in women and also in the rise. Women are more unemployed than men in the labour market. The average duration of unemployment in 1998 was 138 days for women, compared with 119 days for men – an obvious difference! Women's incomes, which I have already mentioned, are of course caused by increased part-time employment and minor employment of women. One of the main reasons, however, is also the family work and childcare done by women. It is interesting to note that the difference between men and women's incomes at the start of the career is much lower than later. This fact indicates that this ever-increasing income difference is due to interruptions in the work process. An exception are the teachers because there is simply more female than male teachers. In this area, income is almost the same. With regard to the young generation, there is a certain hope, for the girls are as well trained as the boys, and that is good. This shows that the better training and the expansion of the professional range help women to have greater opportunities in the future in terms of income and career choice. In all working time models, economic policy aspects played a role in atypical employment relations, family policy aspects are hardly taken into account today. Flexible working time, part-time work, minor employment are applied where the economy needs – families are hardly taken into account. One is flexible in employment, but there is a certain degree of rigidity in Austria as far as family work is concerned. One problem, of course, is the mismanagement of labour market policy promotion measures. In the future, I hope to be more compatible with the work force and family, that family policy aspects will increasingly be involved in working time models and that women will need to be particularly helped in childcare. The aim is to ensure more freedom of choice for women, such as the introduction of a child care check or an educational allowance for women. Increased return aid must be found, targeted and offered to women. The suggestions of the Court of Auditors, which appear in this activity report, must be taken seriously.

For the controlling: This is the only point I do not agree with the Court of Auditors, because the approach that has been adopted is to expect the use of an exercise site according to the number of nights spent. I have already mentioned this in committee. There are not only nocturnal events, but – I am saying this now in the Jargon of tourism – there are also day guests who take advantage of an exercise place. There are even soldiers who need the practice site and don't even enter it. For example, I think of the following example: If the artillery shoots into the exercise area from outside the exercise area, then parts of the site are locked, although there is no one inside at all. In any case, it is very difficult to measure in terms such as soldiers per square kilometer or more. Let's just take a hunter's association as an example that exercises, for example, defense. It comes with a square kilometer, but there are 150, 160 or more people there. A hunting tanker might need 10 or 12 or more square kilometres, but only covers 40 to 50 people. The only point, taking into account the military conditions, that really can be measured for the occupation of an exercise site is a look at the exercise calendar – and that there is, in that there is and has already been a controlling in the past – and the information gives about the strength of the occupation with units in this area, and nothing else. – So far to the question of controlling. In order to provide health services, it is recommended that an emergency medical service be established constantly when shooting weapons. This can also be seen with helicopters. The criticism of the situation regarding the emergency medical service is therefore limited. I am now missing the time to deal with this in detail. A doctor who would be in constant service there is hard to find, because he would not have been burdened there that thanks would not have been paid off, and that would also be a very unsatisfactory activity. The Court of Auditors proposes three options. The Minister has become aware of the last possibility, namely to maintain, to expand and to strengthen the use of the exercise site. This will be done in the future, and I see the necessary consequences drawn from the report.

It has issued a recommendation which is rather rare for the Court of Auditors. It has expressed its support for increasing the number of officials involved in the fight against environmental crime. The Court of Auditors points out that this recommendation – as strange as it may sound for a control body, which has to be taken into account, in particular, that as much as possible is saved – has its right to obtain, in view of the material values to be preserved, because it must not be overlooked that any non-compliance in the field of environmental protection can later lead to enormous costs as a consequence. In addition, one should also not overlook the fact that, of course, it is also necessary to protect intangible values that cannot be quantified at all: for example, this is our water, that is the soil of our homeland, that is the air. We therefore believe that, in this area – not least from the point of view of ensuring comprehensive environmental protection in 1984 – everything must also be put into effect by the bodies concerned, which will ensure that our environment is protected in a sustainable way, whether in the preventive field or in the repressive sector. We are also on the point of view – and have expressed this in our recommendations – that increased cooperation between the preventive and the repressive sphere should be carried out. ParlaMint-AT_2000-03-21-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00016_d7e12893	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! This report clearly reflects the weaknesses of the university, but it avoids a detailed treatment of identified weaknesses. Let me first take a brief look at the women and then at the current state of the universities. How does the woman present herself in her position as a public university? Some headlines: "Women's share in the university: The proportion of women in Uniprofessors is pitiful." – This headline is from the former Minister of Science Dr. Einm. "Among 200 graduates of mechatronic studies at Linz University, only one woman was." "First, a woman completes theology study." "Women at the head of a university college"; "Habilitation should go away so that women come up"; a complete ignorance reflecting demand; on the other hand, of course, rectors are also opposed to the abolition of this habilitation. One sees the contradiction of the topic of woman in study and teaching. An example from my profession. A patient comes and asks: "Sister, where is the Lord Primar?" Answer: "He's standing before you!" – This is a situation that happens very often in my profession. However, this sentence still testifies to a completely outdated idea of the predominance of men in certain professional worlds. But, ladies and gentlemen, not for long! The proportion of students is currently 48 percent. In 2001, it will be 50 percent. Women storm the universities. In Graz, 16 000 students face 12 000 colleagues of the "strong sex". Here you can ask the question freely according to the US model: Where are the guys?, while in the leadership of the universities and certain professional groups one would have to call the other: Where are the girls? But at the end of the 20th century. In the 21st century, ladies and gentlemen, women have taken hold of university teachers, and they will certainly be in the 21st century. In the 19th century, the leaders of these educational institutions also climb up. Now to the universities. The Austrian universities, like most universities in Europe, have developed over several centuries and can refer to a lot of tradition, but also to a lot of scientific and educational substances. They are currently almost alone in academic education and are responsible for about 70 percent of the Austrian research potential. They are an important part of the Austrian identity and have trained most of the leaders in industry and industry after the war. Nevertheless, and above all because of the long SP-dominated education policy, the importance of universities in the public has become increasingly low in recent years. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] The main reason for this is that up to a few years ago, more than 60 percent – I stress: more than 60 percent! – all graduates of the universities found use in the State Service, where the recruitment criterion was primarily the acquired titles and not the acquired knowledge. This has now led to a lack of appreciation of the teaching contents and, on the other hand, has established the award of a title as the main purpose of university education. This situation only changed at the moment when the professional advancement of a graduate was only dependent on acquired knowledge and no longer on the title, as is the case today in the economy and industry everywhere. All of a sudden, the universities now have a responsible task to do, which is difficult for them, because this has not yet been required of them. Moreover, for more than 20 years SP-dominated policies have been trying to reform the universities or adapt them to the needs of time. The reforms began with the UOG in 1975 and continued in 1993 with the new UOG, with a growing desire to reform the legislator, so that the universities today have problems with these reforms and can only carry out their actual task of training students and research more difficult or no longer at all. Thus, since the beginning of the 1970s, there has been a continuous reform at the university, with Austria having since then the highest degree of change and participation at all levels among all universities in the Western world. The political leaders assumed that the high level of co-determination would speed up reforms. This is, in principle, right, but it has been created by several groups working against each other. And in Germany, already in the 1970s, it was recognized that too much participation at all levels hinders functionality, and many of the old 68s rules have been reversed. In Austria, one typically continued determinedly. Studiengesetz, Dienstrecht, UOG were decided independently without mutual vote. We've been warning against it all the time. It is so easy to define the task of a university: It is intended to offer excellent, forward-looking, motivated training and to conduct research at international level. Our state, which has begun to prove itself in a large Europe, so far seems to be more oriented towards the eastern models than the western ones. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Dr. Niederwieser.]] Our competitors and role models can only be universities like those in Bavaria, Switzerland, Holland. These countries have similar traditions, and they have managed to re-establish their universities. But with us, the best graduates, especially those in the technical subjects, leave the country to a large percentage, but not because they like to do so, but because they have little future prospects in Austria. It's very easy to break up, ladies and gentlemen. It is much, much more difficult to rebuild this. I quote Professor Gornik, who says: "Reforms can be created by building on the 30 percent excellence that still exists at our universities." To bring about a change of mood within universities and to enforce the principle of performance without regard to acquired rights is the only possibility that our universities have. And the students and all university teachers must realize that they are sitting in a boat. Because the students are the most important group of the university as they give it the legitimacy of their existence. During the debate on the social situation of the students, I have called the students beggar students. After the university report, this should not be required to roll up his sleeping bag under a bridge.

With regard to the cracks in the concrete vault of the pianist tunnel, the Court of Auditors notes that these were due to a significant deviation of the calculated assumptions and the actual load on the building. – Questions: How were such clear deviations possible? Why were the Asag’s officers able to overlook this and thus take immediate action in a million degrees? How is it possible to allow such deviations in the construction of a building without reviewing the static effects? Furthermore, as already mentioned, it should be clarified whether, in the context of a research contract with a "non-contributory financial contribution to support scientific research in the field of ageing" in a street construction company, there is a legitimate or wrong suspicion of illegal party financing. – Questions: Where and to whom were these grants paid? Is there a result of this research, a study, an opinion or the like? Which scientists have worked on it? What about traffic related to ageing? I imagine that a contract to a transport company should also include something about the link between ageing and transport! – Further question: Who is responsible for this donation?
